Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

read · completed

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.\n\n(End of file - total 36 lines)\n</content>",

        "text": "---\ndescription: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.",

25: +// ExportConfigOverSSH runs command in an SSH exec session at addr (host:port) with password
26: +// authentication and returns its output, trimmed. It serves the kinds whose NOS has no
27: +// scrapligo platform for SaveConfig (MikroTik RouterOS).
28: +func ExportConfigOverSSH(addr, username, password, command string) (string, error) {
29: +	config := &ssh.ClientConfig{
30: +		User:            username,
31: +		Auth:            []ssh.AuthMethod{ssh.Password(password)},
32: +		HostKeyCallback: ssh.InsecureIgnoreHostKey(), // lab node, host key unknown
33: +		Timeout:         10 * time.Second,
34: +	}
35: +
36: +	conn, err := ssh.Dial("tcp", addr, config)
37: +	if err != nil {
38: +		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to connect to %s via SSH: %w", addr, err)
39: +	}
40: +	defer conn.Close()
41: +
42: +	session, err := conn.NewSession()
43: +	if err != nil {
44: +		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create SSH session for %s: %w", addr, err)
45: +	}
46: +	defer session.Close()
47: +
48: +	output, err := session.CombinedOutput(command)
49: +	if err != nil {
50: +		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to execute %q on %s: %w", command, addr, err)
51: +	}
52: +
53: +	exported := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
54: +	if exported == "" {
55: +		return "", fmt.Errorf("received empty configuration from %s", addr)
56: +	}
57: +
58: +	return exported, nil
59: +}
60: +
61: +// WriteSavedConfig writes a saved configuration to <LabDir>/<dir>/<file> of the node, the
62: +// path its startup config is read from on the next deployment.
63: +func WriteSavedConfig(
64: +	cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig,
65: +	dir, file string,
66: +	content []byte,

109: +
110: +// startSSHServer serves password-authenticated exec sessions on 127.0.0.1 and answers
111: +// each command with reply. It returns the address and the commands received.
112: +func startSSHServer(
113: +	t *testing.T,
114: +	user, password string,
115: +	reply func(cmd string) (out string, status uint32),
116: +) (string, func() []string) {
117: +	t.Helper()
118: +
119: +	_, key, err := ed25519.GenerateKey(rand.Reader)
120: +	if err != nil {
121: +		t.Fatal(err)
122: +	}
123: +	signer, err := ssh.NewSignerFromKey(key)
124: +	if err != nil {
125: +		t.Fatal(err)
126: +	}
127: +
128: +	cfg := &ssh.ServerConfig{
129: +		PasswordCallback: func(c ssh.ConnMetadata, p []byte) (*ssh.Permissions, error) {
130: +			if c.User() == user && string(p) == password {
131: +				return &ssh.Permissions{}, nil
132: +			}
133: +			return nil, errors.New("denied")
134: +		},
135: +	}
136: +	cfg.AddHostKey(signer)
137: +
138: +	ln, err := net.Listen("tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
139: +	if err != nil {
140: +		t.Fatal(err)
141: +	}
142: +	t.Cleanup(func() { ln.Close() })
143: +
144: +	var mu sync.Mutex
145: +	var cmds []string
146: +
147: +	go func() {
148: +		for {
149: +			nc, err := ln.Accept()
150: +			if err != nil {
151: +				return
152: +			}
153: +			go func() {
154: +				_, chans, reqs, err := ssh.NewServerConn(nc, cfg)
155: +				if err != nil {
156: +					return
157: +				}
158: +				go ssh.DiscardRequests(reqs)
159: +				for newCh := range chans {
160: +					ch, chReqs, err := newCh.Accept()
161: +					if err != nil {
162: +						continue
163: +					}
164: +					for req := range chReqs {
165: +						if req.Type != "exec" {
166: +							_ = req.Reply(false, nil)
167: +							continue
168: +						}
169: +						var p struct{ Command string }
170: +						_ = ssh.Unmarshal(req.Payload, &p)
171: +						_ = req.Reply(true, nil)
172: +						mu.Lock()
173: +						cmds = append(cmds, p.Command)
174: +						mu.Unlock()
175: +						out, status := reply(p.Command)
176: +						_, _ = ch.Write([]byte(out))
177: +						_, _ = ch.SendRequest("exit-status", false,
178: +							ssh.Marshal(struct{ Status uint32 }{status}))
179: +						ch.Close()
180: +						break
181: +					}
182: +				}
183: +			}()
184: +		}
185: +	}()
186: +
187: +	return ln.Addr().String(), func() []string {
188: +		mu.Lock()
189: +		defer mu.Unlock()
190: +		return append([]string(nil), cmds...)
191: +	}
192: +}
193: +
194: +func TestExportConfigOverSSH(t *testing.T) {
195: +	addr, received := startSSHServer(t, "admin", "pass word#1", func(string) (string, uint32) {
196: +		return "\r\n# export\r\n/system identity\r\nset name=r1\r\n\r\n", 0
197: +	})

202: +	}

204: +		t.Errorf("output = %q, want it trimmed", got)
205: +	}

207: +		t.Errorf("commands run = %q, want [/export]", cmds)
208: +	}
209: +}

235: +			if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), tc.want) {

22: func ExportConfigOverSSH(addr, username, password, command string) (string, error) {
23: 	config := &ssh.ClientConfig{
24: 		User:            username,
25: 		Auth:            []ssh.AuthMethod{ssh.Password(password)},

1: // Licensed under the BSD 3-Clause License.
2: // SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-3-Clause
3: 
4: package nodes
5: 
6: import (
7: 	"crypto/ed25519"
8: 	"crypto/rand"
9: 	"errors"
10: 	"net"
11: 	"os"
12: 	"path/filepath"
13: 	"strings"
14: 	"sync"
15: 	"testing"
16: 
17: 	clabtypes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/types"
18: 	"golang.org/x/crypto/ssh"
19: )
20: 
21: // startSSHServer serves password-authenticated exec sessions on 127.0.0.1 and answers
22: // each command with reply. It returns the address and the commands received.
23: func startSSHServer(
24: 	t *testing.T,
25: 	user, password string,
26: 	reply func(cmd string) (out string, status uint32),
27: ) (string, func() []string) {
28: 	t.Helper()
29: 
30: 	_, key, err := ed25519.GenerateKey(rand.Reader)
31: 	if err != nil {
32: 		t.Fatal(err)
33: 	}
34: 	signer, err := ssh.NewSignerFromKey(key)
35: 	if err != nil {
36: 		t.Fatal(err)
37: 	}
38: 
39: 	cfg := &ssh.ServerConfig{
40: 		PasswordCallback: func(c ssh.ConnMetadata, p []byte) (*ssh.Permissions, error) {
41: 			if c.User() == user && string(p) == password {
42: 				return &ssh.Permissions{}, nil
43: 			}
44: 			return nil, errors.New("denied")
45: 		},
46: 	}
47: 	cfg.AddHostKey(signer)
48: 
49: 	ln, err := net.Listen("tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
50: 	if err != nil {
51: 		t.Fatal(err)
52: 	}
53: 	t.Cleanup(func() { ln.Close() })
54: 
55: 	var mu sync.Mutex
56: 	var cmds []string
57: 
58: 	go func() {
59: 		for {
60: 			nc, err := ln.Accept()
61: 			if err != nil {
62: 				return
63: 			}
64: 			go func() {
65: 				_, chans, reqs, err := ssh.NewServerConn(nc, cfg)
66: 				if err != nil {
67: 					return
68: 				}
69: 				go ssh.DiscardRequests(reqs)
70: 				for newCh := range chans {
71: 					ch, chReqs, err := newCh.Accept()
72: 					if err != nil {
73: 						continue
74: 					}
75: 					for req := range chReqs {
76: 						if req.Type != "exec" {
77: 							_ = req.Reply(false, nil)
78: 							continue
79: 						}
80: 						var p struct{ Command string }
81: 						_ = ssh.Unmarshal(req.Payload, &p)
82: 						_ = req.Reply(true, nil)
83: 						mu.Lock()
84: 						cmds = append(cmds, p.Command)
85: 						mu.Unlock()
86: 						out, status := reply(p.Command)
87: 						_, _ = ch.Write([]byte(out))
88: 						_, _ = ch.SendRequest("exit-status", false,
89: 							ssh.Marshal(struct{ Status uint32 }{status}))
90: 						ch.Close()
91: 						break
92: 					}
93: 				}
94: 			}()
95: 		}
96: 	}()
97: 
98: 	return ln.Addr().String(), func() []string {
99: 		mu.Lock()
100: 		defer mu.Unlock()
101: 		return append([]string(nil), cmds...)
102: 	}
103: }
104: 
105: func TestExportConfigOverSSH(t *testing.T) {
106: 	addr, received := startSSHServer(t, "admin", "pass word#1", func(string) (string, uint32) {
107: 		return "\r\n# export\r\n/system identity\r\nset name=r1\r\n\r\n", 0
108: 	})
109: 
110: 	got, err := ExportConfigOverSSH(addr, "admin", "pass word#1", "/export")
111: 	if err != nil {
112: 		t.Fatalf("ExportConfigOverSSH: %v", err)
113: 	}
114: 	if got != "# export\r\n/system identity\r\nset name=r1" {
115: 		t.Errorf("output = %q, want it trimmed", got)
116: 	}
117: 	if cmds := received(); len(cmds) != 1 || cmds[0] != "/export" {
118: 		t.Errorf("commands run = %q, want [/export]", cmds)
119: 	}
120: }
121: 
122: func TestExportConfigOverSSHErrors(t *testing.T) {
123: 	tests := map[string]struct {
124: 		password string
125: 		out      string

129: 		"wrong password": {password: "nope", out: "x", want: "failed to connect"},

142: 				return tc.out, tc.status

147: 				t.Errorf("err = %v, want it to contain %q", err, tc.want)
148: 			}

It returns the address and the commands received.\nfunc startSSHServer(\n\tt *testing.T,\n\tuser, password string,\n\treply func(cmd string) (out string, status uint32),\n) (string, func() []string) {\n\tt.Helper()\n\n\t_, key, err := ed25519.GenerateKey(rand.Reader)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatal(err)\n\t}\n\tsigner, err := ssh.NewSignerFromKey(key)\n\tif err != nil 41: 	}

54: func (n *vrRos) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
55: 	// Init VRNode
56: 	n.VRNode = *clabnodes.NewVRNode(n, defaultCredentials, scrapliPlatformName)
57: 	n.ConfigDirName = configDirName
58: 	n.StartupCfgFName = startupCfgFName
59: 	// set virtualization requirement
60: 	n.HostRequirements.VirtRequired = true
61: 
62: 	n.Cfg = cfg
63: 	for _, o := range opts {
64: 		o(n)
65: 	}
66: 	defEnv := map[string]string{
67: 		"CONNECTION_MODE":    clabnodes.VrDefConnMode,
68: 		"USERNAME":           n.Cfg.Credentials.Username,
69: 		"PASSWORD":           n.Cfg.Credentials.Password,
70: 		"DOCKER_NET_V4_ADDR": n.Mgmt.IPv4Subnet,
71: 		"DOCKER_NET_V6_ADDR": n.Mgmt.IPv6Subnet,
72: 	}
73: 	n.Cfg.Env = clabutils.MergeStringMaps(defEnv, n.Cfg.Env)
74: 
75: 	n.Cfg.Binds = append(n.Cfg.Binds, fmt.Sprint(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "ftpboot"), ":/ftpboot"))
76: 
77: 	if n.Cfg.Env["CONNECTION_MODE"] == "macvtap" {
78: 		// mount dev dir to enable macvtap
79: 		n.Cfg.Binds = append(n.Cfg.Binds, "/dev:/dev")
80: 	}
81: 
82: 	n.Cfg.Cmd = fmt.Sprintf(
83: 		"--username %s --password %s --hostname %s --connection-mode %s --trace",
84: 		n.Cfg.Env["USERNAME"],
85: 		n.Cfg.Env["PASSWORD"],

87: 		n.Cfg.Env["CONNECTION_MODE"],
88: 	)

92: 	n.InterfaceHelp = InterfaceHelp
93:

97: // SaveConfig overrides the default VRNode SaveConfig to handle MikroTik RouterOS
98: // Uses direct SSH connection since scrapligo doesn't support MikroTik RouterOS platform.
99: // To be refactored to use scrapli's GenericDriver or an enhanced scraplicfg.
100: func (n *vrRos) SaveConfig(_ context.Context) (*clabnodes.SaveConfigResult, error) {
101: 	configContent, err := clabnodes.ExportConfigOverSSH(
102: 		net.JoinHostPort(n.Cfg.LongName, "22"),
103: 		n.Cfg.Credentials.Username,
104: 		n.Cfg.Credentials.Password,
105: 		"export",
106: 	)
107: 	if err != nil {
108: 		return nil, err
109: 	}
110: 
111: 	// Filter out ether1 management interface IP address configuration
112: 	filteredConfig := n.filterManagementInterfaceConfig(configContent)
113: 
114: 	return clabnodes.WriteSavedConfig(n.Cfg, n.ConfigDirName, n.StartupCfgFName,
115: 		[]byte(filteredConfig))
116: }
117: 
118: // filterManagementInterfaceConfig removes ether1 (management interface) IP address configuration
119: // from the exported RouterOS configuration to avoid including containerlab management IP settings.
120: func (n *vrRos) filterManagementInterfaceConfig(config string) string {
121: 	lines := strings.Split(config, "\n")
122: 	filteredLines := make([]string, 0, len(lines))
123: 	inIPAddrSection := false
124: 
125: 	for _, line := range lines {
126: 		trimmedLine := strings.TrimSpace(line)
127: 		if strings.HasPrefix(trimmedLine, "/") {
128: 			inIPAddrSection = trimmedLine == "/ip address"
129: 		}
130: 
131: 		if inIPAddrSection &&
132: 			strings.HasPrefix(trimmedLine, "add address=") &&
133: 			slices.Contains(strings.Fields(trimmedLine), "interface=ether1") {
134: 			continue
135: 		}
136: 
137: 		filteredLines = append(filteredLines, line)
138: 	}
139: 
140: 	return strings.Join(filteredLines, "\n")
141: }

28: 	defaultCredentials = clabnodes.NewCredentials("admin", "admin")
29: 
30: 	InterfaceRegexp = regexp.MustCompile(`ether(?P<port>\d+)`)
31: 	InterfaceOffset = 2
32: 	InterfaceHelp   = "etherX (where X >= 2) or ethX (where X >= 1)"

36: 	configDirName   = "ftpboot"
37: 	startupCfgFName = "config.auto.rsc"
38: 
39: 	scrapliPlatformName = "mikrotik_routeros" //nolint: misspell
40: )
41: 
42: // Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.
43: func Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {
44: 	platformAttrs := &clabnodes.PlatformAttrs{
45: 		ScrapliPlatformName: scrapliPlatformName,
46: 	}
47: 
48: 	nrea := clabnodes.NewNodeRegistryEntryAttributes(defaultCredentials, nil, platformAttrs)
49: 
50: 	r.Register(kindNames, func() clabnodes.Node {
51: 		return new(vrRos)
52: 	}, nrea)
53: }

55: type vrRos struct {
56: 	clabnodes.VRNode
57: }
58: 
59: func (n *vrRos) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
60: 	// Init VRNode
61: 	n.VRNode = *clabnodes.NewVRNode(n, defaultCredentials, scrapliPlatformName)
62: 	n.ConfigDirName = configDirName
63: 	n.StartupCfgFName = startupCfgFName
64: 	// set virtualization requirement
65: 	n.HostRequirements.VirtRequired = true
66: 
67: 	n.Cfg = cfg
68: 	for _, o := range opts {
69: 		o(n)
70: 	}
71: 	defEnv := map[string]string{
72: 		"CONNECTION_MODE":    clabnodes.VrDefConnMode,
73: 		"USERNAME":           n.Cfg.Credentials.Username,
74: 		"PASSWORD":           n.Cfg.Credentials.Password,
75: 		"DOCKER_NET_V4_ADDR": n.Mgmt.IPv4Subnet,
76: 		"DOCKER_NET_V6_ADDR": n.Mgmt.IPv6Subnet,
77: 	}
78: 	n.Cfg.Env = clabutils.MergeStringMaps(defEnv, n.Cfg.Env)
79: 
80: 	n.Cfg.Binds = append(n.Cfg.Binds, fmt.Sprint(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "ftpboot"), ":/ftpboot"))
81: 
82: 	if n.Cfg.Env["CONNECTION_MODE"] == "macvtap" {
83: 		// mount dev dir to enable macvtap
84: 		n.Cfg.Binds = append(n.Cfg.Binds, "/dev:/dev")
85: 	}
86: 
87: 	n.Cfg.Cmd = fmt.Sprintf(
88: 		"--username %s --password %s --hostname %s --connection-mode %s --trace",
89: 		n.Cfg.Env["USERNAME"],
90: 		n.Cfg.Env["PASSWORD"],
91: 		n.Cfg.ShortName,
92: 		n.Cfg.Env["CONNECTION_MODE"],
93: 	)
94: 
95: 	n.InterfaceRegexp = InterfaceRegexp
96: 	n.InterfaceOffset = InterfaceOffset
97: 	n.InterfaceHelp = InterfaceHelp
98: 
99: 	return nil
100: }
101: 
102: // SaveConfig overrides the default VRNode SaveConfig to handle MikroTik RouterOS
103: // Uses direct SSH connection since scrapligo doesn't support MikroTik RouterOS platform.
104: // To be refactored to use scrapli's GenericDriver or an enhanced scraplicfg.
105: func (n *vrRos) SaveConfig(_ context.Context) (*clabnodes.SaveConfigResult, error) {
106: 	// Create SSH client configuration
107: 	config := &ssh.ClientConfig{
108: 		User: n.Cfg.Credentials.Username,
109: 		Auth: []ssh.AuthMethod{
110: 			ssh.Password(n.Cfg.Credentials.Password),
111: 		},
112: 		HostKeyCallback: ssh.InsecureIgnoreHostKey(), // Accept any host key
113: 		Timeout:         10 * time.Second,
114: 	}
115: 
116: 	// Connect to the MikroTik RouterOS device
117: 	conn, err := ssh.Dial("tcp", fmt.Sprintf("%s:22", n.Cfg.LongName), config)
118: 	if err != nil {
119: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to connect to %s via SSH: %+v", n.Cfg.LongName, err)
120: 	}
121: 	defer conn.Close()
122: 
123: 	// Create a session
124: 	session, err := conn.NewSession()
125: 	if err != nil {
126: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create SSH session for %s: %+v", n.Cfg.LongName, err)
127: 	}
128: 	defer session.Close()
129: 
130: 	// Execute the export command to get the configuration
131: 	output, err := session.CombinedOutput("export")
132: 	if err != nil {
133: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to execute export command on %s: %+v", n.Cfg.LongName, err)
134: 	}
135: 
136: 	config_content := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
137: 	if config_content == "" {
138: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("received empty configuration from %s", n.Cfg.LongName)
139: 	}
140: 
141: 	// Filter out ether1 management interface IP address configuration
142: 	filtered_config := n.filterManagementInterfaceConfig(config_content)
143: 
144: 	// Save config to mounted labdir startup config path
145: 	configPath := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.ConfigDirName, n.StartupCfgFName)
146: 	err = os.WriteFile(configPath, []byte(filtered_config),
147: 		clabconstants.PermissionsOpen) // skipcq: GO-S2306
148: 	if err != nil {
149: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
150: 			"failed to write config by %s path from %s container: %v",
151: 			configPath,
152: 			n.Cfg.ShortName,
153: 			err,
154: 		)
155: 	}
156: 	log.Info("Saved configuration to path", "nodeName", n.Cfg.ShortName, "path", configPath)
157: 
158: 	return &clabnodes.SaveConfigResult{
159: 		ConfigPath: configPath,
160: 	}, nil
161: }
162: 
163: // filterManagementInterfaceConfig removes ether1 (management interface) IP address configuration
164: // from the exported RouterOS configuration to avoid including containerlab management IP settings.
165: func (n *vrRos) filterManagementInterfaceConfig(config string) string {
166: 	lines := strings.Split(config, "\n")
167: 	filteredLines := make([]string, 0, len(lines))
168: 	inIPAddrSection := false
169: 
170: 	for _, line := range lines {
171: 		trimmedLine := strings.TrimSpace(line)
172: 		if strings.HasPrefix(trimmedLine, "/") {
173: 			inIPAddrSection = trimmedLine == "/ip address"
174: 		}
175: 
176: 		if inIPAddrSection &&
177: 			strings.HasPrefix(trimmedLine, "add address=") &&
178: 			slices.Contains(strings.Fields(trimmedLine), "interface=ether1") {
179: 			continue
180: 		}
181: 
182: 		filteredLines = append(filteredLines, line)
183: 	}
184: 
185: 	return strings.Join(filteredLines, "\n")
186: }

Reasoning
The PR is a pure refactor: extracts `ExportConfigOverSSH` and `WriteSavedConfig` from `vrRos.SaveConfig` into `nodes/ssh_export.go` plus tests. The behavior is largely unchanged. Things to check:

1. **Error message change**: base used `n.Cfg.LongName` in connect/execute error messages; head uses `addr` = `net.JoinHostPort(n.Cfg.LongName, "22")`. Minor, but fine — actually it's arguably more precise. Not a finding.

2. **Connection address change**: base used `fmt.Sprintf("%s:22", n.Cfg.LongName)`; head uses `net.JoinHostPort(n.Cfg.LongName, "22")`. JoinHostPort handles IPv6 properly. Improvement. But wait: LongName for vr-ros — is it a container name like `clab-x-r1`? Docker container names can't have colons, so fine.

3. **WriteSavedConfig signature**: it takes dir/file rather than full path. Check whether there's an existing helper for this. Let me check whether other nodes have similar SaveConfig write patterns that could reuse `WriteSavedConfig`. E.g. many nodes write configs in their SaveConfig implementations. But the PR only refactors vr_ros, that's fine — surgical change. However, if there's an existing helper like `n.SaveConfigResult` or utils.WriteFile... Let me check `clabutils` for a write-file helper. There's `utils.WriteFile`? Let me grep.

4. **Session.Close error ignored**: fine.

5. **CombinedOutput error with non-zero exit status**: ssh returns ExitError; error message includes the output? In base, "failed to execute export command on %s: %+v" — head keeps same via wrapping %w. Fine.

6. **InsecureIgnoreHostKey**: pre-existing behavior, carried over. Not new.

7. Test: the test server writes the output and sends exit-status before... Actually the ordering: client's CombinedOutput waits for session close/exit status. Server writes output, sends exit-status, closes channel. But note: server never returns from the `for newCh := range chans` loop after `break`; that's fine.

Potential issue in test: after `req.Reply(true, nil)` and writing, the handler `break`s out of chReqs loop, then loops back to `for newCh := range chans` — blocks until channel closes. Fine.

Another: `_ = req.Reply(false, nil)` for non-exec; continue. OK.

One potential real issue: in `TestExportConfigOverSSHErrors`, subtests run sequentially; each starts its own server on port 0. Fine.

Race: `cmds` slice — copy under mutex. Fine.

The accept loop goroutine leaks until `ln.Close()` in cleanup — fine for tests.

8. **Behavioral regression check**: base error path for connection failure included `n.Cfg.LongName` (container name); head error includes `addr` which is `LongName:22`. Slightly different message but fine.

9. **`ExportConfigOverSSH` exported in `nodes` package**: The doc comment says it serves kinds with no scrapligo platform. Naming: `ssh_export.go` in nodes package. Fine.

10. Check that `SaveConfigResult` exists in nodes package. Yes presumably.

11. Check whether `WriteSavedConfig` could clash with existing similar helpers — grep for `SaveConfigResult{ConfigPath` across nodes to see duplication. This is a potential "reuse existing helper" question: is there already a helper that writes the config? Let me grep.

12. Docs: no user-visible behavior change; error message changed slightly ("failed to connect to %s via SSH" now includes port). No docs needed.

13. Schema: no topology change.

14. Test coverage: tests exist and cover main paths. Good.

15. One more check: `net.JoinHostPort` vs `fmt.Sprintf("%s:22")` — if `n.Cfg.LongName` is already a FQDN... fine.

Potential subtle bug: the ssh client's `Timeout: 10 * time.Second` only applies to the TCP dial, not to session execution — pre-existing.

Potential issue: `session.CombinedOutput(command)` — command comes from a fixed string "export" in caller. But `ExportConfigOverSSH` is exported and takes an arbitrary command; if other nodes later pass user-influenced input... not a real issue now.

Now the test file: does it conflict with existing tests in package `nodes`? Check for an existing `startSSHServer` helper or a test helper with same name — collision would break the build. Let me grep for `startSSHServer` in head nodes package. Also check `getCredentials`? Not relevant.

Also check that `ssh_export_test.go` imports `golang.org/x/crypto/ssh` — already a dependency (base vr-ros.go used it). Fine.

Let me grep the head tree for other SaveConfig implementations writing config files, to see whether `WriteSavedConfig` duplicates an existing helper (e.g., something like `SaveConfigResult` used elsewhere with same write logic). And check for `startSSHServer` name collisions.
The PR is a refactor: it extracts the MikroTik RouterOS `SaveConfig` SSH export logic from `nodes/vr_ros/vr-ros.go` into a reusable `nodes/ssh_export.go` helper plus a new test file. Now let me verify surrounding context: existing helpers in the `nodes` package, name collisions, and how `SaveConfigResult` and similar write flows already exist.
